# BACKLOG.md — OTV4TEST — v0.96

**The fork's own backlog. Started BLANK on 2026-09-08 by the operator's ruling:**
*"If you think we could benefit from a backlog it should start BLANK and be
specific to this repo."* Mainline's 3,986-line backlog is not this file and is
not carried; it lives in `TX-9AI/options_trader_v4` where its items are worked.

Same rules as mainline (WORKING_AGREEMENT §18): ships in every archive, one
row per item, a title bump and a PART 3 entry per delivery. Revisions are cited
as `OTV4TEST rN`, never a bare `rN`. States (the LAST cell, which `check_ledger_parity` reads): 🔲 OPEN · ◐ built/pushed, not yet
proven on the box · ✅ closed · ⬛ dead.

**BUILT ≠ PUSHED ≠ BAKED** — and on this box the third means running on the
isolated QQQ instance with the operator watching the plan ledger daily.

---

| **STRK.1** | ⬜ **STRIKE SELECTION SHOULD SCALE WITH THE IMPLIED MOVE, NOT ALWAYS BE NEAREST-OTM.** Operator, 2026-09-21, on the parity-rest design: *"Correct unless we buy far enough out of the money because volume has implied a big move as inbound."* 🔑 He is naming a real gap. VOLT buys the **nearest** OTM strike by his own r72 ruling (*"Select the nearest OTM strike"*), which is correct when the signal says "a move" and wrong when it says "a BIG move" — further OTM buys more contracts, more convexity and a lower probability for the same budget, and the volume gate already produces a CONTINUOUS ratio (live board 2026-09-21: *"volume 1.10x baseline, below the 1.25x prior"*) that could scale it. **THE MACHINERY EXISTS:** `gex_pin_butterfly.expected_move(underlying, atm_iv)` computes EM from the chain's ATM IV with no fallback, and the TCS already reads it. A strike of `spot + k × EM`, with `k` driven by the volume ratio, is expressible today. ⚠️ **AND IT DOES NOT CONFLICT WITH [[REST.1]]** — the parity rest arms only at par delta, so a far-OTM ticket that never goes deep never rests and keeps its full convexity; the rule engages exactly when convexity is already spent. 🔴 **BLOCKED ON SAMPLE, NOT ON DESIGN.** VOLT takes 2-3 real fires a session (see [[volt-trade-rate-churn-inflated]]) and 2026-09-21 was its first working day, so there is nothing to set `k` with. This is the same thing on the EXIT side, for every strategy. **THE DESIGN, from the operator's spec:** the resting sell limit is placed **immediately after the entry fill**, not at 15:40 and not when delta arrives — a 15:30 entry has fifteen minutes and cannot afford to wait for a queue. Priced at **`parity − ε` and NOTHING ELSE**, re-priced each tick. 🔴 **THE TARGET MUST NEVER ENTER THE PRICE, AND MY FIRST CUT OF THIS ROW GOT IT WRONG — the operator caught it: *"Is that essentially a 100% take profit or are we able to get multiples higher?"*** `max(target, parity − ε)` REINTRODUCES the +100% guillotine r82 just removed: on today's VOLT the mark touches the 2.01 target when parity is 2.00, so the limit fills at **+101% for $1,010** against **+491% / $4,910** parity-only — **a $3,900 error on one trade**. ⚠️ `limit_ladder.py`'s own header states the principle that was violated: *"The exit TRIGGER decides WHEN to start closing. It NEVER anchors WHERE the limit sits. That separation is the whole point."* A target is a TRIGGER; putting it in the price is smuggling a trigger into an anchor. 🔑 **PARITY HAS NO CEILING** — it is `spot − strike`, so a 20-point move is a 20.00 limit that re-prices upward every tick and can only be lifted at fair value. This is **not a take-profit; it is a fair-value exit**: delta decides WHEN, parity decides AT WHAT PRICE. Below par delta the order does not rest at all, because parity is not fair value while real extrinsic remains. `limit_at_mark(mark, floor=...)` ALREADY DOES `px = max(px, floor)` — the floor parameter exists and no caller passes it. Every `check_*.py` bootstraps with the parent-directory form; `exit_replay` was the odd one out precisely because it is **not** a `check_*.py` and was therefore never in the sweep (RPL.3). Recorded because the generalisation — *a checker green in a shell and red under systemd certifies the path nobody runs* — is sound and, measured, costs us nothing today. **Reported to the control agent in the direction it came out**, having offered it in either. **(2) `legs_of` never read `option_symbol`** — 25 of 44 rows carry it and every one was refused as "no leg symbols on row". **(3) it queried `streamer_symbol=?` with an OCC string** — trades hold `QQQ   260911C00718000`, `quote_series` is keyed `.QQQ260911C718`; measured, **19 of 24 traded contracts have quotes under the transform (up to 44,771 rows on one) and 0 of 24 under the key it used**. **(4) THE BUTTERFLY CENTRE LEG WAS WEIGHTED −1 AGAINST A POSITION OF −2** (`entry_engine.py:801` builds `(center_contract, 2, -1)`; its own comment says *"the debit is lower + upper − 2*center"*). Pre-existing and **unreachable until (3) was fixed**, because no butterfly had ever resolved a quote. **(5) the closing verdict blamed the tape** and named `s3_push` as the remedy — a service **masked by ruling on this box** (r21/BOX.1) that must never run. `if not counts:` is true exactly when `refused` is populated, so it fired four lines below a block headed *"these are the tool's gaps, not the tape's"* and contradicted it as the LAST thing printed. **(6) THE POSITIVE CONTROL MODELLED THE WRONG EXIT RULE** — it replayed every trade under a hardcoded 25% premium stop. Measured, most of this book never exited on one. It surfaced as a **false alarm on a winner**: a `GEXPinButterfly` that held to `hard_close_15:45_ET` for **+$1,630** was replayed under a 25% stop, exited at −$130, and the control reported the TOOL as suspect. ⚠️ And the obvious regex is a trap — `(\d+)%` matches the `pnl=-26.5%` tail. 🔑 **THE GENERAL RULE, WORTH MORE THAN THE BUG: A DEAD INSTRUMENT HIDES THE DEFECTS IN EVERYTHING IT MEASURES.** Repairing one does not reveal them singly — it reveals them all at once, which is precisely when the validity check nobody has been reading becomes load-bearing. **ALSO FIXED:** orientation derived via `is_credit_vertical` (real columns, §22) instead of the writer-less `is_short_position` (MEAS.2 — 0 of 44 rows set it, so the flip was dead code and every CREDIT position took the LONG sign, reading its wins as losses); the OCC root regex widened to `[A-Z][A-Z0-9]*` for split-adjusted contracts (`GOOGL1`); and the docstring corrected — it claimed a `symbol`-prefix fallback *"only when it resolves to EXACTLY ONE contract"* that **has never existed in the function**, and must not be built, because r36 subscribed the underlying so `quote_series` now holds a row keyed exactly `QQQ` and a prefix match would replay the UNDERLYING'S PRICE PATH as a premium path. **RESULT: 0 → 10 of 44 replayed, and the accounting balances (10 + 34 refused = 44).** The 34 are named: 18 rows predate the `option_symbol` column, 15 have a contract whose quotes begin AFTER the trade closed (the chain is subscribed later), 1 bad timestamp. ⚠️ **THE HONEST CEILING IS 10, NOT 25** — independently measured, only 10 of the 25 rows carrying `option_symbol` have quotes covering their own window. ⚠️ **AND THE CONTROL NOW COVERS ONLY 2 OF THE 10**, with the other 8 reported NOT APPLICABLE by name — not a pass and not a failure. | **RPL.2** | ⬜ **r62'S OWN REPAIR NEEDED THREE REPAIRS, TWO OF THEM MINE.** **(1) THE STOP REGEX HAD NO WORD BOUNDARY.** r62 shipped `(?:hard_stop|premium_stop|stop)_(\d+)%` SEARCHED, so the match walked through any prefix: measured, `tcs_stop_15%_of_credit` → 0.15 and `trail_stop_10%` → 0.10, both read as premium stops they are not. Latent (we emit neither) but `orb_trail_stop` exists and a percentage on it is one line away. Anchored to `\b…(\d{1,3})%(?!_of_)`. 🔑 **THE LOOKAHEAD IS A BACKSTOP AND NOT THE FIX, AND THAT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED** — the mainline agent deleted `(?!_of_)` from its own copy expecting red and got GREEN, because with `\b` the character before `stop` in `tcs_stop` is `_`, a word character, so the boundary alone rejects it. **Its mutation of its own guard is what told us to look for a boundary we did not have; an outcome-only report would have transferred nothing.** **(2) THE BAND SCALED WITH THE RULE UNDER TEST — MY REGRESSION.** r62 derived `rec_stop` per row, correct for the RULE and wrong for the BAND: the expression multiplied the tolerance BY the stop and the `0.25 × 4` cancelled only at 0.25, so deriving the stop silently made the check **looser on wider stops** — 28% of entry cost at a 20% stop, 35% at 25%, **56% at a butterfly's 40%**. A wider stop meaning a looser check is backwards. Keyed on entry cost alone now. Found by the control agent in its tree and reported here before we had noticed. **(3) A CONTROL THAT APPLIED TO ZERO ROWS PASSED SILENTLY.** *"0 did not reconcile out of 0 applied is the most convincing possible zero and means nothing at all."* It shouts UNVERIFIED now, and every run states the applied count — currently **2 of 10**. ⚠️ **`RECONCILE_TOL` = 0.35 IS LEFT ALONE AND IS RECORDED AS AN UNFITTED PRIOR.** §31: never tested against outcomes. Measured 2026-09-19, the eligible set is **n=2** with deviations of **1.5% and 2.1% of entry cost against bands of 35% and 56%** — roughly 20× looser than anything observed. That is a MECHANISM, not a threshold (§12): two observations cannot set a tolerance, so only the SHAPE was corrected. ⚠️ **AND THE BAND CHANGE IS NOT BEHAVIOURALLY OBSERVABLE ON THIS BOOK** — both the old and new bands are far wider than any measured deviation, so no verdict flips either way. It is proven by arithmetic and by mutation, not by an outcome, and the gate's header says so rather than letting X9 be read as covering it. 🔑 **ONE THING r62 GOT RIGHT, CONFIRMED:** deriving the stop per row cut the ATPButterfly's reconciliation error from **$79.7 to $11.0** — the derived rule is the correct one. | ◐ r63 |

| **RPL.4** | 🔴 **r62'S ORIENTATION REWIRE INVERTED EVERY CREDIT VERTICAL, AND r62'S LEDGER ROW CLAIMS IT AS A FIX. THIS CORRECTS THE RECORD.** `legs_of` assigns `short_symbol` **−1** and `long_symbol` **+1**, so a credit vertical's combo is `long − short` = **−(spread value)** — which RISES as the spread cheapens, i.e. as the seller wins. **The multi-leg signs already encode favourable orientation.** r62 applied `flip = -1 if _is_credit(row)` on top and inverted it. **MEASURED, one variable, the book's only replayed credit row:** `pnl_usd −56.0` against a replayed **+28.0** — the book lost, the replay profited. After r64: **−28.0**, right sign. The nine other replayed rows are byte-identical either way. ⚠️ **AND IT WAS ACCIDENTALLY CORRECT BEFORE r62.** `is_short_position` has no writer (MEAS.2), so `flip` was `+1` for every row the tool had ever seen — exactly what the spread columns need. **r62 replaced a dead flag that was right by accident with a live resolver that was wrong on purpose**, and its ledger row records deriving orientation from real columns as a repair. It repaired nothing and introduced this. 🔑 **SURFACED BY THE MAINLINE CONTROL AGENT, WHICH HAD THE IDENTICAL REWIRE STAGED AND UNLANDED** — blocked by a permission classifier rather than by diligence, as it said plainly. Reproduced here before it was believed. **THE FIX:** the flip is scoped to the single-leg `option_symbol` path, the one place orientation is not already encoded. On this tree **no path we actually trade needs a flip at all** — credit vertical, butterfly `1/−2/1` and single-leg debit are all already favourable-positive; only a naked short would need it, and `OptionsSignal.is_valid` fails closed on one. Kept **guarded and dead** rather than deleted so nobody re-derives why it is absent, with the reachability claim written at the site because *"cannot fire today"* is exactly what silently stops being true. ⚠️ **r62's SELFTEST ENCODED THE DEFECT AS A REQUIREMENT** — it asserted a credit and a debit vertical with the SAME legs must differ, which is only true while the wrong flip is applied. **Rewritten to the contract that replaced it rather than loosened** (the r33 precedent). 🔴 **AND OUR CONTROL IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THIS CLASS.** Not one credit row is eligible: our sweeps exit on `sweep_breach_accepted`, which is not a premium stop, so the control correctly excludes every one. It applies to **2 of 10 replayed rows and both are debits**. **A control that can only test premium-stop exits can never say anything about the sweep or the TCS, whose exits are entirely structural.** The control agent measures the same shape at 68% NOT APPLICABLE on its own book. The naming is the gain; the silence remains. **No answer tonight and none invented — this is the limitation, recorded as one.** **GATE:** `check_exit_replay` X11–X13, **born red 2 of 13** at 9116102 with the inverted signs printed verbatim. **X11 asserts PER LEG BY SYMBOL, never a sorted sign list** — `[-1, 1]` is the same list whichever leg carries which, which is why the control agent's equivalent check stayed GREEN on the restored defect. **X13 DRIVES the guarded branch with a synthetic single-leg credit row**, because a mutation disabling its marker changed nothing observable and a gate resting on real rows stayed green on it (measured). A spec written from that documentation mangled its own paths — and the two directives then failed in OPPOSITE directions: `POS` greps with `!`, so a missing file FLAGS (**measured: this refused the r61 archive, every POS reading MISSING**); `NEG` greps without `!` and swallows the error, so a missing file reads as *"the string is absent"* and **the assertion passes**. 🔑 **A `NEG` IS THE ONLY THING PROVING SUPERSEDED CODE IS GONE**, so a vacuous one certifies that without looking — §15 v1.3's own finding (*"one failed open and one failed closed… a gate that can do either is unrelated to the thing it claims to check"*) recurring in the PARSER rather than in the pattern it was written about. **FIXED BOTH HALVES, because either alone leaves a hole:** one `_spec_val` normaliser for POS/NEG/CHECK/DEL and the REV/DESC/REPO/LEDGER header fields, **and** an unresolvable POS *or* NEG path is now a REFUSAL rather than a silent pass — normalising alone would still let a TYPO'd path pass a NEG in silence. **GATE:** `check_land_spec_parse.py` L0–L5, **born red 3 of 6** at 71b89ab, and it **DRIVES THE REAL LANDER** end to end (§21 — reading the source proves nothing about what bash does with it). 🔴 **L0 IS A SAFETY GUARD THAT RUNS FIRST AND ABORTS THE FILE:** `land.sh` finds its target by scanning `"$HOME"/*/` for the spec's REPO markers, and the REAL checkout carries `docs/GENESIS-TEST.md` — so every case runs with **HOME redirected into a scratch tree** under markers that exist nowhere else, and L0 refuses to proceed if the lander ever names a repo outside it. Without it, `oi_proxy = 1000·gamma/mark` gives `gex = 100,000·gamma²·spot/mark` — **QUADRATIC**. Measured: 449 of 534 contracts carry real OI, so **~85 strikes per cycle are on the quadratic branch**. 🔑 **AND GAMMA PEAKS AT THE MONEY, SO THE QUADRATIC BRANCH AMPLIFIES EXACTLY THE STRIKES THAT DECIDE `pin_strike` AND `pin_conc`** — which `GEXPinButterfly` (requires PINNING) and `ATPButterfly` (pin proximity) gate live entries on. `data/open_interest.py`'s own docstring already argues the principle: *"unavailable and zero are different facts, and conflating them is what produced a gamma-squared surface labelled as gamma exposure."* The proxy conflates them. ⚠️ **HISTORICALLY THIS WAS THE WHOLE CHAIN, NOT 16%** — until OTV4TEST r6 wired the fetch, OI was 0 everywhere and GEX was a pure gamma² surface; mainline's warehouse shows the same step at 2026-08-21 (0.0% → 34% → 84%). ### Measurement defects — a wrong number is worse than no number

| id | item | state |
|---|---|---|
| **MEAS.1** | 🔴 **`pnl_pct` IS DIRECTION-BLIND, SO EVERY CREDIT TRADE REPORTS ITS LOSSES AS GAINS.** `database/trade_logger.py:706` — `pnl_pct = (exit_price - entry_prem) / entry_prem`, premium-relative and unsigned by direction. Correct for a debit; for a short credit vertical premium UP is a LOSS, so the sign inverts. **Measured: 6 of 6 SweepCreditSpread rows on this box disagree in sign with their own `pnl_usd`** (e.g. `pnl_usd −90.00` with `pnl_pct +0.1775`). ⚠️ **THE DAMAGE IS TO STUDIES, NOT TRADES** — verified by grep that the STORED column has no reader in the decision path (`exit_engine.py:1052` computes its own live value; `management.py` passes that around). It corrupted a fleet strategy table on 2026-09-17, showing the sweep at median **+16.67%** on a strategy that lost money. ⚠️ **THE OBVIOUS FIX IS A TRAP:** `is_short_position` is populated on every row and reads **0 on all six sweeps**, so keying on it would silently fix nothing. **DERIVE from `pnl_usd`**, which arrives at `log_exit` already direction-aware (§22 prefers deriving): `mag = abs(exit-entry)/entry; pnl_pct = mag if pnl_usd >= 0 else -mag`. **NOTHING IS LOST** — `pnl_usd`, `entry_premium` and `exit_premium` are all stored, so every historical row (local AND in S3, which is a faithful copy of the same wrong value) is recomputable. **SCOPE: the defect is in the WRITER, so it is on every box and mainline carries it too.** ⚠️ **SIGN ONLY:** the magnitude stays return-on-premium, which for a credit spread is return on CREDIT not on RISK (−$57 on $893 credit reads −6.4% when the risk was $1,007). That is a design call and is the operator's. Ruled 2026-09-17: lands with the Saturday work. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **MEAS.2** | ⬜ **`is_short_position` IS WRITTEN AND NEVER SET.** Populated (0 nulls across 34 rows) and **0 on every credit trade**. A column that exists, is written, and carries no information — the §22 hazard in its quietest form. Either set it at the record builder or delete it; a half-live flag is worse than none, because the next reader will trust it. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **MEAS.3** | 🔴 **`_check_fed_day()` HAS ALWAYS RETURNED FALSE — TWO INDEPENDENT PARSE DEFECTS, SILENT.** `data/macro_data.py:172`. (1) `datetime.strptime(ev_date_str, "%b %d, %Y")` expects `"Jun 21, 2026"`; the live feed returns ISO `"2026-09-16T14:00:00-04:00"` → `ValueError` → `continue`, every event every day, so the loop never reaches the impact test. (2) It requires `event.get("currency") == "USD"` and **the payload has no `currency` key** — measured 2026-09-16, 105 events, keys `['title','country','date','impact','forecast','previous']`. Either alone is sufficient. **Silent by construction**: the `ValueError` is swallowed by `continue`, so nothing logs — §0.5. ⚠️ The r125 comment AT THE SITE warns that *"`Fed Day: No` on an FOMC morning is a wrong answer that reads exactly like a right one"*; the timezone half was fixed while the parse underneath was dead. **MEASURED: `is_fed_day = 0` on all 34 rows ever written here**, and `FED_DAY_ORB_BOOST = 0.20` has never once applied. **CARRIED FROM v3** — the file header names `options_trader_v3/data/macro_data.py`, so mainline and the fleet have it too. ⚠️ **NOT A TRADING RULING:** the operator's position (2026-09-17) is that the trades are news-agnostic — *"We're not trading the news. We're trading price action."* So this is a RECORD defect: a flag we stamp on every row and that has never been true. Fix it or delete it; do not leave it reading false. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **MEAS.4** | ⬜ **`vix_at_entry` AND `adx_at_entry` ARE ZERO ON 27 OF 34 ROWS**, including all seven trades of 2026-09-16, while VIX candles sit in the feed store. Same class as MEAS.2 — a recorded field nobody can rely on. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **MEAS.5** | ⬜ **`surface_series.gex` IS CHAIN-WIDE, WRITTEN 195× PER TICK, AND PER-STRIKE GEX IS NEVER PERSISTED.** `derived/surface.py:133` reads `net_gex` off the `GEXSnapshot` — the chain total — and writes that one value onto every strike row (195 of them on 2026-09-16, 121,743 rows). **Summing it is wrong** and I did exactly that before checking, reporting +13.8B where the figure was +71M; the sign and the zero crossing survive a constant multiple so the finding held, but the description did not. ⚠️ **CONSEQUENCE: no post-hoc study of pin STRUCTURE is possible** from this table or from the warehouse — a gamma FLIP LEVEL cannot be computed, only the chain net through time. The strategies read per-strike data live from the snapshot, so nothing is broken today; what is lost is the ability to ever study it. | 🔲 OPEN |

(2) A test of the **NEAR** edge registers the touch. (3) Acceptance beyond the **FAR** edge is the breach, and **retires the ENTIRE cluster, every member with it** — not only the edge accepted through. Each has its own purpose in its own time… what differentiates the breakout from the liquidity hunt, while both of them originate from the ORB, the liquidity hunt has an identified target and the breakout has to identify an EXHAUSTION SIGNAL to get out before it gives back."* Three ORB-born trades, three different exits by construction: **RUNAWAY exits on THESIS** (the 50 is lost); **HUNT exits at its TARGET** (the resting pool it named at entry); **BREAKOUT HAS NO TARGET AND MUST EXIT ON EXHAUSTION.** ⚠️ **AND r44's TRAIL STUDY IS THE ARGUMENT FOR WHY THIS MATTERS MOST HERE:** a trail returns a median 42.9% of MFE, so a strategy with NO TARGET leaning on a trail as its primary exit is the worst possible pairing of the three. **(2) MOMENTUM DIVERGENCE** — a new run-favourable extreme while 5m rate-of-change is WEAKER than at the prior extreme, *"the move is continuing on fumes"*, which EXITS. 🔑 **AND ITS OWN NOTE-TO-FUTURE-SELF NAMES THE OPERATOR'S PREFERENCE:** a stricter *"mode 3"* requiring BOTH signals to agree before exiting was *"intentionally deferred… maps closer to how the operator actually trades — you don't bail on divergence alone if the move isn't also stretched"*, left as a code change *"by the operator's request."* **BRK.1 is the trade that finally has a reason to make that change**, and the hook the comment names is the `_exhausted` combine step. **WIRING THIS COSTS NO NEW LOGIC** — it is a dead path with a live purpose.<br>🔑 **THE PLAN MANAGES THE POSITION TOO — OPERATOR'S RULING, 2026-09-18:** *"I like the thought of the plan also managing the position, kind of the way the condor does already."* He asked the architecture question directly — *"the plan feeds the strategy, the strategy executes, but what manages the position once it's in motion, is that also the plan?"* — and the answer was already his own, from 2026-08-27 (`strategy/management.py` v2.0): **THE PLAN DECIDES; THE EXIT ENGINE CALCULATES AND EXECUTES.** So BRK.1 is **THREE files, not two**: `breakout_plan.py` (entry — searches, selects, publishes trigger params), `breakout.py` (declares bars, confirms), and a **coverage entry in `strategy/management.py`** emitting one `Intent` per tick under `Breakout/manage`. ⚠️ Today that module covers Runaway, both butterflies and a standalone vertical; **ORB, the formed condor, the tent and adopted positions are explicitly NOT covered** and run their own paths — so adding Breakout is joining a live pattern, not inventing one.<br>🔑 **AND `Intent` ALREADY HAS THE EXACT SHAPE THE TWO-TIER EXHAUSTION NEEDS** — `HOLD / CLOSE / TRAIL`, with a reason, a named condition and an optional trail level, converted by `to_exit_decision()`. Extension-from-midline emits **TRAIL** (tighten, do not exit); momentum divergence emits **CLOSE**. **And HOLD carries the operator's other requirement**, 2026-09-18: *"I would like the plan to be watching closely for either scenario… I want the plan to know that the pool might be the end or we may be able to keep going and it should be watching for a sign of either."* That is a HOLD whose reason names the lean, every tick — e.g. `approaching pool 719.70 (n=4, 0.31 away); depth holding, aggression decaying -> leaning END` versus `pool breached, depth thin, aggression sustained -> leaning THROUGH`.<br>🔴 **G3 IS DEMOTED. VOLUME EXPANSION DOES NOT SEPARATE A BREAK THAT MAKES 1R FROM ONE THAT DOES NOT — MEASURED 2026-09-18, n=736.** Every warehouse `ohlc` object back to 2026-07-08, fleet-wide. Break = first 1m CLOSE beyond the 5-bar opening range; risk R = the break bar's opposite extreme (the structure stop); "worked" = reached **+1R before −1R**, the operator's own definition. **Base rate 50.9%** — a symmetric test landing on a coin flip, which is the classifier's sanity check. Four baselines, all the same answer:<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`ORB mean (all 5)` med **0.62** worked vs **0.59** stopped, best lift 1.05x<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`ORB mean ex-09:30` med **0.95** vs **0.93**, best lift 1.11x (n=39)<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`ORB median ex-09:30` med **1.00** vs **0.99**, best lift 1.02x<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`prior 5 bars` med **1.13** vs **1.04**, best **1.25x → 55.3% vs 50.9%, lift 1.08x (n=266)**<br>🔑 **AND THE OPERATOR PREDICTED THIS BEFORE THE STUDY RAN.** 2026-09-18: *"one of the characteristics of a fakeout is taking out stops and then turning the other way."* **Stops are market orders, so the harvest MANUFACTURES the volume spike** — which means break-bar volume describes THAT A BREAK HAPPENED, never WHETHER IT HOLDS. ⚠️ **His comment arrived while the study was being designed and changed it.** The first design would have measured volume, found weak separation, and concluded the THRESHOLD needed tuning; the correct reading is that the GATE cannot work, and no threshold rescues it. **If G3 survives at all it is the prior-5-bars baseline at ~1.25x as a TIEBREAKER, worth four points, never a filter.**<br>🔑 **AND THE SAME LOGIC PROMOTES THE OTHER THREE:** the pool, the gamma regime and depth are the gates a stop run CANNOT manufacture. That is now the whole entry thesis, and all three are still unmeasured.<br>🔑 **A FAKEOUT IDENTIFIED IN ADVANCE IS NOT A REFUSAL — IT IS A DIFFERENT TRADE. OPERATOR, 2026-09-18:** *"if we identify that it's a fakeout before it happens, maybe we can tip off the appropriate trade to take that."* **`execution/handoff.py` ALREADY IS THAT MECHANISM** — *"the handing-off strategy writes an EXPLICIT TOKEN: from X, to Y, at level L, side S, good for N ticks"* — and today only the hunt grants, only to the sweep, only on a rejection. ⚠️ **r43 FREED IT TO MEAN MORE:** the grant was built as a SLOT WORKAROUND (*"the slot rule yields for that strategy only"*) and non-compete DELETED the slot rule, so the token outlived its original purpose and is available for a better one. **THE BREAKOUT PLAN SHOULD THEREFORE EMIT ONE OF THREE VERDICTS, NOT A BOOLEAN:** **TAKE** (gates pass → breakout fires); **FADE** (the gates fail in the way that says HARVEST — a pool sitting right where the stops are, a pinning regime, depth refilling after the sweep — → **grant to the sweep, side reversed**, so the same evaluation that refuses the breakout ARMS the fade); **WAIT** (ambiguous → no trade, no grant). 🔑 **The breakout and the fade are ONE SETUP READ TWO WAYS**, which is why one plan can decide both and why the gates stop being a filter and become a router.<br>🔑 **BREAKOUT IS THE FIRST RESPONDER, AND IT DEFERS RATHER THAN BLOCKS — OPERATOR, 2026-09-18:** *"when we go back to hierarchy based, Breakout should be ahead of liquidity hunt, and if breakout determines that the trade is actually shaping up to be a liquidity hunt it should defer to that trade immediately."* ⚠️ **AND THAT IS WHY THE GRANT IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN A CONVENIENCE: HIERARCHY *IS* THE CASCADE r43 REMOVED.** In a priority order, "Breakout defers to the hunt" would normally mean the hunt is NEVER ASKED — precisely the failure r43 measured, where the runaway claimed the slot and TrendCreditSpread, GEXPinButterfly and ATPButterfly went unevaluated for **thirty-five minutes**. **The handoff token is what makes a deferral REACH the other trade instead of silencing it**, so the fleet's hierarchy posture depends on it in a way this box's non-compete posture does not. 🔑 **BREAKOUT GOES FIRST BECAUSE IT IS THE EARLIEST EVALUATOR** — it sits at the break bar, before the move has gone anywhere, which is the only moment the triage is still cheap. The hunt's setup, price travelling to where orders rest, cannot be judged until price is already travelling. **SO THE VERDICT SET HAS FOUR DESTINATIONS:** **TAKE** (open air, trending regime, depth thin → Breakout fires); **DEFER → HUNT** (a pool sits in the path and the move will REACH it → grant to the hunt, which has the target); **DEFER → SWEEP** (the break is a harvest that turns HERE → grant to the sweep, side reversed); **WAIT** (ambiguous → no trade, no grant). 🔑 **The two deferrals are the operator's own taxonomy applied at entry:** the hunt takes the move that GOES SOMEWHERE AND STOPS because it has an identified target; the sweep fades the level that NEVER GAVE WAY AT ALL. ⚠️ **GRANT TARGETS MUST BE EXTENDED FOR THIS** — `handoff.live()` is consulted today only by the sweep (`_attempt_sweep_on_grant`), so the hunt needs its own consult, and `grant()` needs Breakout as a `from_strategy`.<br>🔑 **G7 IS THE FIRST GATE WITH EVIDENCE, AND IT IS A BINARY, NOT A DISTANCE — MEASURED 2026-09-18, n=327.** Same break definition, `liquidity_ledger` joined per session; **only `is_named` levels used, because PDH/PDL/R-n/S-n and overnight structure exist at the OPEN by construction** and a level the ledger discovered at 11:00 was not knowable at a 09:47 break. Base 1R rate 51.4%. **OPEN AIR (no named pool ahead): 63.0%, lift 1.23x, n=46. POOL AHEAD: 49.5%, lift 0.96x, n=281.** ⚠️ **n=46 CARRIES A STANDARD ERROR NEAR 7 POINTS, so the 13.5-point gap is roughly 2 SE — SUGGESTIVE, NOT ESTABLISHED.** It is the strongest signal found so far and the opposite shape to the volume result: that was a conclusive negative on n=736, this is a promising positive on a thin sample. ⚠️ **AND THE DISTANCE BANDS DO NOT BEHAVE, SO THEY ARE NOT USED:** 0–0.5R gives **60.9%**, BETTER than 0.5–1.0R at 48.5% — backwards from *"a pool right there stops you"*. Most likely contamination: a named pool within 0.5R of the break is probably THE LEVEL THAT FORMED THE ORB BOUNDARY, so those are breaks that ALREADY CLEARED a pool rather than breaks toward one. **G7 therefore stands as OPEN AIR vs NOT, and earns a distance threshold only on more sessions.**<br>**OWED BEFORE ANY THRESHOLD BECOMES A NUMBER:** a study on the banked tape and S3 giving the distributions for imbalance, `tagged_frac`, the volume multiple, the regime boundary and the depth-depletion ratio — **separating breaks that ran from breaks that reverted**. Same job as the `THESIS_BAND_WICKS` multiplier (r44), which is a prior and not a measurement. ⚠️ **NO THRESHOLD IN THIS SPEC IS A NUMBER YET, DELIBERATELY** — r39's width and r44's trail lock were both set from measurement, and picking these from priors would be the one habit this repo keeps paying to unlearn.<br>🔑 **G4 IS MEASURED AND IS THE ONLY ENTRY GATE WITH REAL SEPARATION — 2026-09-18, n=255, APPLIED TO LIVE CODE AT r53.** Same break definition and same *"worked = +1R before −1R"* rule as the volume and pool studies; signed `order_flow.aggression()` imbalance over the 3 minutes from the break bar, in the break direction, streamed from the warehouse across 255 (sym, date) pairs carrying prints. **Base 1R rate 52.5%.** The two populations are genuinely different — **reached 1R: p25 −0.023, med +0.074, p75 +0.165; stopped: p25 −0.073, med +0.013, p75 +0.099** — medians roughly **6x apart**, and the stopped population's own median sits barely above zero:<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`−0.20` n=232 (91%) **52.6%** 1.00x · `−0.10` n=213 (84%) **54.0%** 1.03x · `+0.00` n=155 (61%) **58.7%** 1.12x<br>&nbsp;&nbsp;`+0.05` n=124 (49%) **62.1%** 1.18x · **`+0.10` n=83 (33%) 65.1% 1.24x** · `+0.15` n=62 (24%) **61.3%** 1.17x · `+0.20` n=38 (15%) **65.8%** 1.25x<br>🔑 **THE CURVE IS MONOTONE THROUGH +0.10 AND THAT IS WHAT MAKES IT CREDIBLE** — unlike the volume study, where every threshold sat on the base rate and the one apparent hit (2.00x, 1.27x) was n=17. **`FLOW_IMBALANCE_MIN` = +0.10**, taken as the best-supported point that still keeps a third of the breaks. ⚠️ **THE PRIOR WAS 0.15 AND THE DATA MOVED IT DOWN** — 0.15 measures 61.3%, BELOW 0.10 — so the guess was in the right neighbourhood and slightly too strict. The dip at 0.15 and the recovery at 0.20 are almost certainly noise at n=62/n=38; **the honest reading is a plateau from +0.10 upward, not a peak at +0.10**, and 0.10 is chosen for sample retention within that plateau rather than because the curve has a maximum there.<br>🔴 **AND THE OTHER HALF OF G4 HAS NEVER ONCE BOUND: `tagged_frac` MEDIAN 1.00, p10 1.00.** Every print on this feed carries an aggressor side, so a 0.60 floor **cannot fire on any observed session** — it is decorative today. ⚠️ **IT IS KEPT ANYWAY AND RELABELLED, NOT DELETED:** the reasoning that put it there is still correct (untagged volume stays in the denominator by design, so a degraded tape would otherwise fabricate conviction), so it stands as **insurance against a feed that stops tagging**, explicitly marked in `GATES` as measured-never-binds. 🔑 **A GATE THAT HAS NEVER GATED MUST SAY SO** — this is the [[fomc-detector-dead]] shape exactly: a condition that is always true and a condition that is never evaluated look identical from downstream, and the only defence is writing down which one it is while it is known.<br>**SO THE ENTRY THESIS NOW HAS ONE MEASURED GATE, ONE SUGGESTIVE GATE AND ONE DEMOTED GATE:** G4 flow at **1.24x on n=255** (measured, applied); G7 open-air at **1.23x on n=46** (suggestive, ~2 SE, held as a binary); G3 volume **dead at n=736** (demoted to tiebreaker at best). **STILL UNMEASURED AND STILL PRIORS: G5 the gamma regime boundary and G6 the depth-depletion ratio** — and G6 remains unreadable on this box for a structural reason worth restating, that **`Quote` is a last-value event with no history**, so book depth cannot be studied from the warehouse at all and only forward-recorded snapshots (r51's `book_depth` field) will ever answer it. | ◐ r51 |
| **ADM.2** | ✅ **(r50) DISSOLVED, NOT DECIDED — THE OPERATOR RULED OUT THE BLOCKING PARADIGM ITSELF.** 2026-09-18: *"In normal (live) trading, one would assume each trade would gate another. I don't like that paradigm. I envision every trade to be 'right' in its own time, as long as our entries are weighing the right information… two separate trades, even potentially trading opposite directions, may have been right at the time that they fired… If we write our plans properly and we write our strategies properly, a given set of conditions for entry may be right at a particular time and I don't see any reason to stop it from firing if it cleared the bar, even if the thesis is the opposite of an existing open trade."* 🔑 **THE COLLISION BECOMES UNREACHABLE RATHER THAN ADJUDICATED.** With `blocked_by` empty permanently, a grant can never contradict a block, so none of the three candidate rulings below needs taking. **r35's named sets stay in place, config-toggleable and simply never armed** — which is what a named-set design is for, and why this reversal costs nothing. 🔑 **AND THE GRANT CHANGES MEANING, FOR THE BETTER.** Under hierarchy a grant was PERMISSION — an override of a block. With nothing to override it becomes **INFORMATION**: *I evaluated this break and concluded it is a harvest; the fade is yours if you want it.* The receiver still clears its own bar. That is strictly more useful than an override and is closer to what BRK.1 wants from Breakout→hunt (r48). ⚠️ **WHAT DOES NOT DISAPPEAR — IT MOVES FROM PERMISSION TO CAPITAL.** His argument is about CORRECTNESS and it holds: two setups can both be right at the moment they fire. But the constraint hierarchy was accidentally supplying was **EXPOSURE**, not correctness. Four strategies at full risk-per-trade is 4x one, and r44 gave the ORB a full-budget slice where it previously took 3%. **The catastrophic cap is now the ONLY thing between 'everything cleared its bar' and a very large day** — which is answerable with a number rather than a hierarchy, and is his to set. 🔑 **AND IT IS SMALLER THAN THE ARITHMETIC SUGGESTS:** two opposite-direction trades on one underlying are partly a HEDGE — gross risk 2x, net directional exposure much less. ⚠️ **WHAT STILL NEEDS COORDINATING, AND IS NOT HIERARCHY: `max_open_of_type`** (sweep 2, everything else 1). That is not one trade gating another, it is a strategy declining to stack on ITSELF, which his reasoning does not cover and which stays. **THE ORIGINAL QUESTION, KEPT FOR THE RECORD:** Operator, 2026-09-18: *"it's gonna be non-competing for the next couple of weeks, but after that we will be going back to a hierarchy based and there will be some blocking between the trades."* 🔑 **THE TABLE IS ALREADY BUILT FOR THAT HALF** — r35 made `blocks` and `blocked_by` NAMED SETS, empty by his ruling, overlaid from `config.ADMISSION_RULES`, so re-introducing blocking is a CONFIG CHANGE AND NOT A REVISION. That was the whole point of the design. ⚠️ **WHAT IS NOT SETTLED IS THE COLLISION.** `execution/handoff.py` was written when the slot rule was binary: a live grant made *"the slot rule yield for that strategy only"*, and **the slot rule no longer exists** (r43). When `blocked_by` comes back, a grant and a block can name the same pair and DISAGREE — and that is not hypothetical, because BRK.1 has Breakout DEFERRING TO THE HUNT BY GRANT (r48) while hierarchy would plausibly have one block the other. **Three candidate rulings, all defensible, and it is the operator's call: (a) the grant WINS — an explicit token from the blocking strategy is consent, and blocking is only the default; (b) the block WINS — a grant answers the slot question only, which is what its own docstring says, and the slot question is gone; (c) the grant wins ONLY when `from_strategy` is the blocker itself**, so A-blocks-B plus A-grants-to-B admits B, while C's grant cannot override A's block. **(c) is the narrowest and matches the grant's original intent most closely.** ⚠️ **DECIDE IT WHILE IT IS CHEAP.** Today both mechanisms are inert — blocks are empty and the only grant path is hunt→sweep — so the collision is unreachable and costs nothing to settle. On the first hierarchy session it becomes a live ordering question with money on it, and the failure mode is silent: the deferred-to strategy simply never fires and nothing says why. **OWED: a born-red checker covering all three pairs (grant only, block only, both) whichever ruling is taken.** | ✅ r50 |
| **ADM.1** | ✅ **(r43) THE OPEN-BRANCH BLOCK IS THE LAST OF r35's SEVEN MECHANISMS STILL DECIDING WHAT MAY OPEN.** 🔑 **OPERATOR'S RULING 2026-09-18, WHICH SETTLES IT AND WIDENS IT:** *"I want the orb, hunt, breakout & sweep all able to fire & non-competing. We only have one session at a time so we need to maximize every opportunity on these setups. In live trading, we will revert back to hierarchy-based."* ⚠️ **THE POSTURE IS TEST-BOX-ONLY AND THAT IS THE POINT** — non-compete exists to harvest the maximum number of comparable setups from ONE session, not because hierarchy is wrong. The fleet reverts to hierarchy, and because the whole table overlays from `config.ADMISSION_RULES` (r35), that reversion is a CONFIG CHANGE AND NOT A REVISION. 🔴 **AND IT IS BIGGER THAN REMOVING A GATE: `attempt_new_entry`'s `signal` CASCADE *IS* THE COMPETITION** — one variable, five `if signal is None` guards, winner-take-all, so the first strategy to fire consumes the tick. Non-competing means each ADMITTED strategy is asked and executed independently. 🔑 **THE PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS AND IS PROVEN IN PRODUCTION:** `_attempt_hunt` (r12) asks its own plan and executes ADDITIVE, taking no slot — *"the hunt runs beside the ORB, never in its slot"*. The conversion is to that template, not to something new. ⚠️ **AND IT DELETES HAND-ROLLED CAPS:** `_attempt_hunt` carries its own *"a hunt is already open on this box"* check, which is `max_open_of_type: 1` doing the same job twice — two mechanisms for one rule is how they drift. ⚠️ **CONCURRENCY CEILING RISES:** worst case four directional debits in one session at full risk-per-trade, with sweep's cap at 2, and Breakout makes five entry types. | `main.py`'s tick loop still reads `if not pos_mgr.has_blocking_position(): attempt_new_entry(...)`, so a box holding an ORB or runaway debit refuses every credit entry — **against the operator's ruling of 2026-09-17**: *"to the greatest extent possible I do not want to set a maximum number of open positions… I WANT sweep to be able to fire while we have an active runaway in progress. We will rely on the runaway to exit gracefully using ITS OWN stops."* The admission table's per-type caps (sweep 2, everything else 1) are built to replace it and `blocks`/`blocked_by` are named sets, empty by his ruling. 🔴 **WHAT BLOCKS THE DELETION, MEASURED AT r40 RATHER THAN GUESSED:** removing the gate orphans `_attempt_butterfly` and `_attempt_sweep_on_grant`, whose only callers are the three direct calls in that same branch — and those are **not** duplicates of `attempt_new_entry`'s own dispatch. `_attempt_butterfly(additive=True)` is r161's EXEMPT path, asked every tick of its slot with a fire APPENDED to whatever is open; `attempt_new_entry`'s butterfly sits behind `elif signal is None:` and competes for the slot. Running both asks three strategies TWICE A TICK; deleting the three loses the exemption that cost MU, NFLX and TSLA a full credit session on 2026-08-31 (r197). ⚠️ **THE `additive` ARGUMENT IS ALREADY A NO-OP IN THAT BRANCH** — r197 made it a fact (`if additive or _pm.has_open_position()`) — so the de-duplication is tractable; it is the ASK-ORDERING that needs deciding, not the record-keeping. **§36 CATEGORY: FOUNDATIONAL** — it changes what may be open simultaneously, which §38.3 reserves to the operator. **NOT ATTEMPTED UNATTENDED:** found at 23:05 on a box that wakes into a trading session at 08:00. | ✅ r43 |
| **PRE.5** | 🔲 **THE WAREHOUSE'S CANDLE SERIES ABOVE 1m ARE SYSTEMATICALLY PARTIAL — A MAINLINE DEFECT, FOR THE CONTROL AGENT, NOT THIS BOX.** Found 2026-09-18 while measuring the zone width on SPX and MU. **`warehouse/s3_push.py:886`**: `SELECT * FROM candles WHERE symbol=? AND interval=? AND ts_epoch_ms > ?` — `push_candles` advances a **high-water mark on the BAR'S OWN TIMESTAMP**. Once a bar is pushed its stamp sits at or below the watermark **forever**, so every later `UPDATE` to that same row — which is what a 1h bar *is* for the 59 minutes after its first tick — **is never pushed again.** Whichever state the pusher happened to catch is what the warehouse keeps. ⚠️ **CORRECT FOR 1m, WRONG FOR EVERY LONGER INTERVAL:** a 1m bar is written once; 5m, 15m, 1h and 1d are mutable rows. 🔑 **AND THE SAME FILE ALREADY HAS THE RIGHT PATTERN** — `push_trades` at `:613` pushes *"every trade row whose CONTENT has changed since last push"*, content-based CDC. `push_candles` uses a timestamp watermark, and for a mutable row those are not the same thing. **MEASURED, every 1h object in `raw/candles` across 31 partitions:** SPX has **ZERO complete bars since 2026-08-21** — median push lag **−59.5 minutes**, i.e. every bar pushed ~30s after its hour OPENED and never again, four weeks of opening ticks; QQQ is partial but survivable at 11–16 complete of 28–44 rows per bar-date, so its coverage is thin rather than absent. ⚠️ **MEASURED-ON-ONE-BOX-ONLY, AND THE CONTROL AGENT'S INDEPENDENT READ DISAGREES (r65).** It measured **QQQ 0 of 21 complete** on 2026-09-16/17/18 — every symbol it sampled fully frozen, not SPX-shaped at all. Ours may have come from an earlier window. **The discrepancy was deliberately NOT bought**: both sides agreed it changes no conclusion — recoverability is settled per-interval by the fifteen-box fan-out and the 08-21 start date is confirmed independently on both sides — and a dedicated S3 run is the ~3,700 sequential `get_object` sweep §38.1 warns about. **The honest state of this figure is one box, one window, not a fleet property.** ⚠️ **NOTHING ABOUT SPX IS BROKEN** — its box pushes 192 objects a day, trades/EOD/plan_ledger all current through 09-17. The defect simply lands on its 16-rows-a-day cadence every time. ⚠️ **THE SILENCE IS THE COST:** any control-side report reading warehouse candles above 1m has been reading opening ticks some invisible fraction of the time, and it renders as a slightly-wrong number, never an error — the plausible-silence class this repo keeps finding in its own code. **OWED: a heads-up to the control agent with the evidence** (`s3_push.py:886`, the −59.5min SPX lag since 08-21, the `push_trades` contrast at `:613`). **NOT OURS TO FIX** — the brief is explicit that mainline is only touched for a significant flaw found here, and this is the "surface it" half, not the "change it" half. **Operator's ruling, 2026-09-18: address it on a weekend.** ⚠️ **AND IT NARROWS A CLAIM I MADE THE SAME NIGHT:** the zone-width replication on SPX rests on July–August backfills only, so the width is replicated on QQQ and MU cleanly and on SPX's older data — a smaller sample than I first presented. 🔑 **DISCHARGED 2026-09-19: DELIVERED TO THE CONTROL AGENT, AND IT CAME BACK STRONGER.** It verified the mechanism independently in otv4 r389 **by reading** (`s3_push.py:926` the hwm load, `:929` the bound SELECT, `:935`→`:945` the watermark advance) and found a better contrast than ours: **`push_trades:636`'s own docstring states the reasoning `push_candles` needed and did not get** — *"Trade rows MUTATE — written at entry, rewritten at exit"* — one function above the one that needed it. **That is what moves this from a design trade-off to a defect.** 🔑 **THEIR SCOPE NOTE, CREDITED, AND IT RESCUED ONE OF OUR OWN RESULTS:** `raw/candles` and `raw/ohlc` are **different pushers** — the file-based one at `:871` keys its ledger on a **content sha**, so the `ohlc` prefix is **UNAFFECTED**. **CONSEQUENCE: r47 STANDS.** Our volume-expansion study (n=736, back to 2026-07-08) read `ohlc`, and it is load-bearing — it is the measurement that ruled volume expansion does not separate a breakout, and therefore why the Breakout's gates are a router rather than a filter. Had it been built on watermark-frozen bars the whole gate design reopened. **It was not, and we would not have known to check without their paragraph.** 🔴 **THE REPAIR IS NOT FORWARD-ONLY, AND THERE IS A DEADLINE.** Their first reading was that a CDC fix could not heal the frozen bars because the store is a pruned rolling window (`push_candles`' own docstring). **Measured here instead** — `RETENTION_DAYS` at `warehouse/retention_purge.py:172`, against `feed_store.db` depth on 2026-09-19: `1m` floor 09-14 (policy 5d, exact) · `5m` floor **09-09** (policy 10d, exact) · `15m` floor **09-02** (policy 20d, **SHALLOWER**) · `1h` floor **07-21** (policy 60d, exact) · `1d` floor **08-10** (never purged). **1h reaches a month BEFORE the 08-21 corruption, so the rows that would supply the correct values were never pruned** — a CDC fix or a one-time watermark reset above 1m heals the existing range, not merely future bars. **CLOCK: the 08-21 bars age out of the 60-day window around 2026-10-20**, rolling forward daily after that. 5m (08-21→09-09) and 15m (08-21→09-02) are **already partly gone**. 🔑 **TWO GENERAL RULES THIS PRODUCED, AND THEY OUTLIVE THE DEFECT.** (1) **`NEVER_PURGE` is a statement about the purge, not about the data** — our `1d` series has no purge clock and still has a floor, at 08-10, because that is when collection began; on a box whose daily collection started after 08-21 there is nothing to recover and the policy would never say so. (2) **The policy is a ceiling on depth, not a promise of it** — `15m` measured three days shallower than its own window. **Every row of a recoverability table must be measured per box per interval or it is an upper bound wearing a fact's clothes.** ⚠️ **THEIR PARTIAL-REPAIR WARNING, WHICH IS THE BEST POINT EITHER SIDE MADE:** a watermark reset on `5m` would re-push correct rows for the last ten days and **nothing** for 08-21→09-09, leaving the bucket holding a mix of healed and frozen bars **with nothing distinguishing them** — worse than uniformly bad data, because it destroys the one property telling a reader which rows to distrust. **And the marking must be computed BY the repair, not appended after it**: an operation that knows which rows it could not heal already holds everything the marker needs, and a second pass is the thing that does not happen. **The marker is per symbol per interval**, carrying that series' own floor. ⚠️ **CONSTRAINT ON OUR OWN RESEARCH SURFACE, recorded because it binds every future study:** anything we read from `raw/candles` **above 1m** over the affected window is reading frozen opening ticks. `raw/candles/interval=1m` is safe; `raw/ohlc` is safe; everything else above 1m carries the caveat until the bucket is repaired or marked. **We do not push, so we contribute nothing to the corruption — we only read it**, which is the asymmetry that explains why this fork found it and mainline did not. **STILL OWED TO US:** their fifteen-box `SELECT interval, MIN(ts_epoch_ms), COUNT(*) FROM candles GROUP BY interval;` fan-out, **read 1d-floor-first** — a 1h gap announces itself as a deadline, a 1d floor after 08-21 announces nothing at all. Blocked on their side by a dead Bash tool, not by a decision. | ◐ heads-up DELIVERED 2026-09-19 — the repair is mainline's |
| **PRE.3** | ⬜ **`level_event` HAS NO HOME ON MAINLINE — FOR THE CONTROL AGENT, NOT THIS BOX.** The rejection fact (OTV4TEST r3) created `level_event` and placed it in this repo's `NEVER_PURGE` (`warehouse/retention_purge.py:249`), so it accumulates here forever. It does not exist on mainline **at all** — no table, no `DERIVED_TABLES` entry, no `DERIVED_ARTIFACT_DAYS` row, no coverage policy. Under a supersede IN PLACE the fork's code inherits mainline's infrastructure, and a table in no list is the by-absence exposure mainline has already paid for three times (r86, r191, r270 — *"added with its push, not after it"*). Infrastructure is mainline's to protect; this row exists so the item is HANDED OVER rather than assumed. Same question applies to any other fork-only store at merge time. | 🔲 OPEN |

### The generated maps, and what the box actually runs

| id | item | state |
|---|---|---|
| **MAP.1** | ✅ (r18) **THE WRITE MAP WAS BLIND TO A TABLE NAMED THROUGH A CONSTANT, AND CALLED FOUR MORE "(unattributed)".** `derived/base.py` writes `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS {STATUS_TABLE}`; every pattern in `gen_write_map.py` requires a literal, so **`derived_engine_status` — written by every derived engine on every tick, read by `tools/manifold_health.py:246` — appeared nowhere**, while the flag list reported *"No writer (0): none"* and the header reported 29 tables. The document whose stated job is to make an unwritten table visible was blind to a writer with no literal, and it **rendered as a smaller plausible number rather than an error** — the defect shape §35 names. Also resolved, all four verified against the live files on the box: `gate_disposition`/`plan_check`/`plan_tick` → `derived_store.db`, `resting_orders` → `resting_orders.db`, **a fourth database the map had never named**. 30 tables now, no `(unattributed)` section. ⚠️ `--check` was rc=0 the whole time: **current and accurate are different questions**, and only the first one is gated. | ✅ r18 |
| **CFG.1** | ✅ (r18) **THE LIVE PIERCE FLOOR CAME FROM A `getattr` DEFAULT WITH AN ORPHAN BESIDE IT.** `derived/levels.py` read `getattr(_cfg, "SWEEP_CS_MAX_REJECTION_PCT", 0.0025)` and **that key was not defined in `config.py` at all**, so every pierce band on the box ran on the literal — while `SWEEP_MIN_REJECTION_PCT = 0.003` sat in config with **zero readers**. The predecessor found this same shape at **15x**; ours is 1.20x, which is exactly why nobody saw it. Fixed by DEFINING the key at the value already running (no behaviour change, the knob becomes real) and labelling the orphan in place so the next reader does not tune the dead one. `LEVEL_ACCEPT_CLOSES` added the same way, so LVL.4's acceptance count moves without a redeploy. ⚠️ **THE CLASS IS NOT CLOSED:** `SWEEP_CS_REJECTION_FRESH_BARS` is read by `strategy/sweep_plan.py` and is likewise undefined in config, so SWP.1's "config-overridable" is only true if you know to add a key that is not there. Sweep it properly — every `getattr(_cfg, ...)` in the tree — as its own row. | ✅ r18 |
| **LAND.1** | ⬜ **A GENUINE RE-ISSUE CANNOT PASS THE LAND GATE.** §26 mandates that re-issuing a revision that ALREADY SHIPPED suffixes it — `r185_r2`, `r185_r3`. `tools/check_land_discipline.py` finds the ledger row with `^\|\s*\*\*(r\d+[a-z]?)\*\*\s*\|`, which matches `r18` and `r18a` and **cannot match `r18_r2`**. So the gate that enforces §35 refuses the exact revision id §26 requires: the lander appends the row, the checker reports *"no row for r18_r2 — a revision absent from the ledger did not happen"*, and the land dies with the payload loose in the tree. **Hit for real on 2026-09-13** while landing this revision. ⚠️ **IT FAILS CLOSED, WHICH IS THE SAFE DIRECTION** — and that is why nobody has found it: the `_rN` path has simply never been exercised, so a rule this repo has carried since 2026-08-19 has never once been landable. Not urgent here, because this revision was a re-cut of an UNLANDED archive and therefore keeps its own number (the r12 precedent) — but a real post-land correction will hit it head-on. One character in the regex; the fix needs a born-red fixture with an `_r2` row. | 🔲 OPEN |
| **HYG.8** | ⬜ **A FULL CHECKER SWEEP CONTAMINATES ITSELF THROUGH ONE SCRATCH DB.** Running all ~110 `tests/check_*.py` with a single `OT_TRADES_DB`/`OT_DERIVED_DB` pair turned `check_audit_20260823` red; **re-run alone with fresh scratch files it is GREEN on both HEAD and the build.** One checker's writes are another's starting state. r13/HYG.6 pointed the env vars at scratch so no checker can reach a LIVE store — correct and unchanged — but it says nothing about checkers reaching *each other*. The lander is barely exposed (it runs only a half's few `CHECK` lines); a full sweep is. Give each checker its own scratch pair, or the next sweep's red set cannot be trusted — and an untrustworthy red is the CV.1 failure that teaches a reader to skip reds. ⚠️ Also seen: `check_land_tooling` is red at HEAD but green in a detached worktree, an environment artifact rather than content. Both recorded so the next sweep starts from a known baseline: **14 pre-existing reds at 98eea8d.** | 🔲 OPEN |

Can I get added to that 'Claude is up' ... and 'Agent not available' if it fails."* Ruling on the mode, same day: **`--continue`, with fallback** to a fresh brief-bootstrapped thread, and the alert names WHICH came up. 🔴 **THE HARD PART IS THE WORD "UP", AND BOTH OBVIOUS CHECKS ARE WRONG — MEASURED ON THE LIVE SESSION.** `devtools.sh` launches `"claude ...; exec bash"`, so `#{pane_current_command}` reads **`bash` while Claude is running fine** (pane 1727 = bash, claude is child 1731) — a check on it reports NOT AVAILABLE over a working session, §17's alarm that cries wolf — and the tmux session **outlives a dead claude** because `exec bash` keeps the pane, so "the session exists" reports UP over a crashed agent, the laundered green §18 names. Availability is therefore **a live `claude` process in the session's process tree**, verified after a settle poll; B1/B1b drive both halves and the negative control was confirmed by creating a real session with no claude in it. ⚠️ **THREE FURTHER TRAPS, ALL MEASURED:** `Linger=no` on this box; a systemd unit reads **no profile**, so `devtools.sh`'s own `command -v claude` resolver **would fail at boot** on a box where the binary plainly works by hand (it sits at `~/.local/bin/claude`) — the §40 shape, a claim about behaviour being a claim about an environment; and `HOME` must be explicit or the credentials in `~/.claude` are never found. ⚠️ **ORDERED `Before=optionsbot.service` AND THAT IS MEASURED, NOT PREFERRED:** the bot's STARTED alert fires **11 seconds after boot** (boot 12:00:10 UTC, alert 12:00:21), which is less than it takes to raise and verify a session, so without ordering the field would read "unknown" on essentially every boot. **IT IS ORDERING, NEVER A DEPENDENCY (§29):** no `Requires`/`Wants`, the raiser exits 0 even on total failure, and `TimeoutStartSec=45` bounds the worst case — so the most this can do to trading is delay the 08:00 start by 45 s, ninety minutes before the open. **THREE ALERT STATES, NOT TWO:** `up (<mode>)`, `NOT AVAILABLE (<reason>)`, or `status unknown` when the stamp is absent or stale, because a missing field and a failed lookup must not look alike (§0.5, r22's own rule for the IP beside it). No new message and no second credential store (§18a) — the bot already holds the token. ⚠️ **AN AVAILABLE SESSION IS NOT A WORKING AGENT.** §38.7 is untouched: a resumed thread sits at a prompt and does nothing until the operator types. 🔲 **WHAT IS NOT PROVEN AND CANNOT BE FROM HERE:** whether the tmux SERVER survives systemd's cgroup handling after the oneshot exits. `Type=oneshot` + `RemainAfterExit=yes` is chosen for exactly that reason, but it is a RUNTIME property no gate reading unit text can establish — **verify on first install** (`systemctl start optbot-claude-boot.service; tmux ls; python3 tools/claude_boot.py --status-only`). ### The operator's tooling

| id | item | state |
|---|---|---|
| **MBX.1** | 🔲 **A SHARED S3 MAILBOX BETWEEN THIS FORK AND THE MAINLINE CONTROL AGENT — DESIGNED 2026-09-19, NOT APPROVED, NOT BUILT, NOTHING EXISTS.** Proposed at the **mainline operator's instruction**; it needs **both** operators' sign-off and an IAM change, and neither agent requests the grant for the other. **SHAPE:** prefix `mailbox/` — deliberately **outside `raw/`**, which has a standing never-delete rule and must not acquire a second purpose. Layout `mailbox/to=<recipient>/from=<sender>/<sent_utc>-<msg_id>.json`, acks at `mailbox/ack/to=<original_sender>/<msg_id>.json` so each side lists exactly ONE prefix for its own receipts. Envelope mirrors the warehouse `_wrap` idiom plus `in_reply_to`. **The mailbox is TRANSPORT, not an archive** — 90-day lifecycle, and **anything that matters is landed into the RECEIVING side's own ledger by the receiving side**, because the record must outlive the lifecycle. 🔑 **OUR OBJECTIONS, ALL ACCEPTED BY THE OTHER SIDE.** (1) 🔴 **IT SOLVES DURABILITY AND AUDIT BUT NOT LATENCY, AND LATENCY IS WHERE 2026-09-19's ENTIRE VALUE CAME FROM** — a defect was surfaced and verified across four files in twenty minutes, and a wrong inference corrected in one round; through a polled mailbox both are days apart or never, against a clock expiring 10-20. **Nothing polls a mailbox**, and §38.7 says plainly that an assistant does not run continuously. So a **session-start LIST of one's own inbox prefix is a REQUIREMENT**, in the handoff reading order beside the transcript read — one LIST, no timer, no grant beyond the read. (2) **Both channels, different jobs** — the live session route for work where latency IS the value, the mailbox for durable handoff and session-death survival; a replacement would trade the thing that worked for the things that failed. (3) **`provenance` mandatory, and PER-CLAIM** — `measured` / `read` / `reported` / `inferred`, with the envelope pinned to the **weakest value present** as a backstop. The day produced two retractions and both were **an inference sent in the register of a measurement**; and `reported` is ours, because a third-party fact relayed onward is none of the other three and unlabelled hearsay is how a number launders itself into a permanent record. (4) **IAM tightenings:** Put scoped to the **other** side's inbox only (neither side can write its own inbox or overwrite the other's sent objects, so forged inbound is impossible), **no `DeleteObject` for either agent at all** (an agent that can delete can erase the audit trail), and an **explicit Deny on `s3:PutObject` for `raw/*`**, because our read grant is bucket-wide and an Allow scoped to `mailbox/*` is worth only as much as the absence of a second Allow nobody has audited. 🔴 **WHAT IT WOULD COST US, AND IT IS THE OPERATOR'S RULING AND NOT THE ASSISTANT'S.** It needs `s3:PutObject` on `vertigo-warehouse-tx9ai`. **Our r31 grant is READ-ONLY and that is recorded as a SAFETY RULING, not a permission detail** (§38.3): this box does not push **because another QQQ box already does**, and a second pusher into `raw/.../sym=QQQ/` would not error — latest-wins by `pushed_at_utc` would silently overwrite the live box's rows in every fleet report. ⚠️ **That reason does not apply to a `mailbox/` prefix — there is no collision there — but the ruling as written is broader than the reason that produced it, and narrowing a safety ruling is the operator's call.** Nothing here implies unmasking `s3-push.service`, and the assistant would object if it did. **If the operator declines, the existing session channel plus the ACK convention is a working answer and the other side has been told so in those terms.** | ◐ r65 — **OUR OPERATOR HAS RULED: WANTED, DEFERRED.** 2026-09-19, verbatim: *"I want the mailbox, however this is working well for the moment. Perhaps during RTH we would tackle something like that."* **Not a decline and not a yes-now.** The live session channel proved sufficient across a full day of defect exchange, which is itself evidence for the original objection — the mailbox solves durability and audit, and latency is where the value turned out to be. Nothing built, nothing in the bucket, no IAM requested. His telemetry precondition stands unchanged whenever it is built. **Mainline's operator has not ruled; their agent has been told ours has, and that the ruling is "yes, later".** Revisit during RTH. |
| **PRE.5** | ✅ (r18) **DELETE `docs/HANDOFF_NOW.md` ONCE IT HAS BEEN READ.** It is a snapshot of r17 by construction; a stale one-time note is worse than none, because the menu hands it to every new thread until it is gone. **Read 2026-09-13 and removed in the same revision** (`DEL` in the land.spec, so the deletion runs before the maps regenerate — the v1.6 ordering). Its content is not lost: what was still open went into the rows below, and the note itself stays in git history. | ✅ r18 |

## PART 3 — CHANGELOG

**v0.96 — 2026-09-22 — OTV4TEST r93 — THE 1-R RAMP NORMALISED THE SIZE OF A
LOSS AND WAS BLIND TO ITS FREQUENCY.**
r91 restored the operator's ramp and it worked — `BUDGET binds` on the first
fire of the day, +$2,725 by 10:19. Then it found its blind spot. 📊 **MEASURED
10:14-10:25 ET:** five Breakouts whose structural stop sat **inside a single
1-minute bar** — 0.075 / 0.050 / 0.190 / 0.225 / **0.010** against a measured
median 1m range of **0.4188** — at 55 to 235 contracts and ~$10,000 each, for
**-$3,089**, taking the day from +$2,725 to +$553. 🔑 **THE ARITHMETIC IS THE
FINDING:** risk per contract falls as the stop tightens so size rises, while
the probability of being hit rises too — nothing in the rule sees it. Stop → 0
gives size → budget cap and P(hit) → 1, so **expected loss converges on the
whole risk budget with certainty.** The operator's model is right and this is
its missing precondition: tight is only safe while the stop is *outside the
noise*. ✅ **REFUSED, BY HIS RULING** — §36 FEASIBILITY, never relaxed.

**v0.64 — 2026-09-19 — OTV4TEST r59 — THE FADE ROUTE WAS DEAD FOR THE WHOLE RESEARCH WINDOW, AND THE ADMISSION RULES WERE VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.**
🔴 **`fading` READ `prep.unmet`, WHICH IS EMPTY ALL WINDOW.** With every acceptance at `any` nothing is ever unmet, so `fading` was permanently False and **`DEFER->HUNT` / `DEFER->SWEEP` could not fire** — the exact twin of the `pooled` bug fixed three lines above it, which I had already caught and then failed to generalise. ⚠️ **IT WOULD HAVE COST THE FORTNIGHT'S FAKEOUT ARM SILENTLY**, and it surfaced only because the operator said what the two weeks is FOR: *"I'm looking for more successful alternatives to the orb trade & hoping the breakout & the hunt can be tailored for both scenarios — either a genuine breakout with volume behind it, or a standard fakeout liquidity grab — both of which have not treated the orb favorably."* 🔑 **ROUTING IS NOT ADMISSION.** Admission asks *may this trade fire* and is deliberately wide open right now; routing asks *what shape is this break*, which is a fact about the tape and must keep reading the FITTED dials. `accepts_fitted()` added; R12 pins it.
🔑 **AND THE PAIRED-SAMPLE POINT, WHICH MAKES THE FORTNIGHT WORTH MORE THAN ITS TRADE COUNT.** r55 recorded that ~9 trades cannot fit a dial — true, and it understates what the sample CAN do. **The operator's own invariant makes the observations PAIRED**: every ORB trade is preceded by a Breakout trade on the same break, so the two weeks yields *same setup, with and without the retest*, and the setup variance cancels. **Nine paired comparisons can rule on the retest even though nine independent trades could not.** Three buckets: **both fired** (paired — does waiting help?); **Breakout only** (the retest never came and the ORB sat out); **ORB only**, which is **IMPOSSIBLE under the invariant** — if it ever occurs live it is a divergence alarm, and worth treating as one.
✅ **THE ADMISSION RULES THE OPERATOR RESTATED ARE ALL ALREADY TRUE — CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED.** *"Every trade fires if it can produce a valid plan… we might have 8 open trades at once & that's fine… one of each type may fire, exception: the sweep can have 2 (condor), and each butterfly type gets one attempt per session."* **(1)** `attempt_new_entry` runs in BOTH arms of the tick (r43/r44) — the open-position branch manages first and then still enters; it gates only on the daily loss limit and RTH, with no `has_open_position()` early return. **(2)** There is **no global position cap** anywhere — only per-type rules. **(3)** `_DEFAULT_RULES` is exactly his table: `max_open_of_type=1` everywhere, `SWEEP=2`, `GEXFLY`/`ATPFLY` at `max_tries_per_session=1`. **(4)** *"if we have a call (vertical) sweep, only a complimentary put (vertical) sweep can complete the condor — and vice versa"*: `IronCondorStrategy.authorize()` returns `need = "put" if have == "call" else "call"`, both-open returns nothing-may-fire, and `generate_signal` REFUSES on `authorized_side` when `prep.side != required_side`.
**MEASUREMENT: 122 of 132, zero regressions.** check_breakout_research v1.2, 17 checks.
**LAND THEN BAKE.**

**v0.61 — 2026-09-19 — OTV4TEST r56 — DID r44's ORB SIZING FIX REACH THE BREAKOUT? YES, AND ONLY SINCE YESTERDAY.**
🔑 **THE OPERATOR ASKED THE RIGHT QUESTION AND THE ANSWER WAS NOT OBVIOUS.** r44's risk scalar lives **inside `_size_geometry`**, so it reaches any strategy that reaches that function — and Breakout reached it for the first time at **r55**, when the strategy-name gate in `main.py` was replaced by a `sizes_on_geometry` declaration. It engages because `stop_premium()` is a **base-class method** (`entry_premium × (1 − stop_loss_pct)`), so every long debit has a non-zero one; r44's scale is skipped only when the stop premium is 0, where *"geometry decides, exactly as it did before r44."*
🔴 **AND THE DANGEROUS PART IS THAT THE OLD PATH AGREED.** Measured: ORB **10** contracts and Breakout **10**, both `orb_geometry`; the pre-r55 `_size_budget` path returns **10 as well** on the same inputs, and the pre-r44 no-stop-premium case returns **3**. **A different rule that coincides is not the same rule**, and nothing would have reported the day it stopped coinciding. R11 therefore pins **the rule and the count**, not the count alone.
⚠️ **WHAT "RISK-NORMALISED" ACTUALLY MEANS HERE, STATED PLAINLY:** r44 scales off the **premium** stop (25% of premium), not the structure stop distance — when the risk scale engages, `geometry_wanted` is computed and **not used**. That is r44's design and it is identical for both trades, so Breakout matches the ORB, which was the question.

**v0.60 — 2026-09-19 — OTV4TEST r55 — BREAKOUT IS THE ORB WITHOUT THE RETEST, AND EVERY INFORMER IS A DIAL READING "any".**
🔑 **THE OPERATOR SPECIFIED IT IN FOUR MESSAGES AND EACH ONE NARROWED THE DESIGN.** *"Have it trade every break that gets a 1-minute candle acceptance beyond the boundary, stop distance is the extreme of the impulsive candle that registered the break, sized the same as the orb, informers are just observers for 2 weeks."* Then: *"Make it follow the orb structurally, but without a retest. And with better informers."* Then the invariant: *"if we do get an orb trade a breakout trade should've preceded it because it's the same trade but without the retest."* And finally the shape: *"I still want the informer set as triggers in the strategy package, but set the acceptance to 'any' for each of those informers that will emerge as dials in 2 weeks."*
🔴 **MY FIRST CUT BYPASSED THE TRIGGER MACHINERY AND HE RULED AGAINST IT, CORRECTLY.** It added an `observe_only` set that made the informers SKIP `cond()` entirely. **A bypass has to be un-bypassed later, and the code that un-bypasses it is code nobody has run.** His shape is better and is what landed: every informer is **still a declared trigger**, the plan still refuses to form unless all of them clear, the strategy still re-checks the PERSISTENT ones on the firing tick — **what widened is the ACCEPTANCE BAND**, one per informer, each reading `any`. 🔑 **AND THE FIT NEVER NEEDED THE BYPASS:** every informer's CONTINUOUS value is already recorded each tick, so any threshold can be fitted from the values themselves — strictly more than a stored would-have boolean would have given.
🔑 **THE INVARIANT IS WHY THE ACCEPTANCE MUST BE "any" AND NOT MERELY "wider".** Breakout precedes every ORB trade only while its binding bars are a **SUBSET** of the ORB's. ⚠️ **AND THE ORB HAS NO WIDTH BAND, NO CLEANLINESS RULE, NO POOL RULE AND NO R FLOOR** — measured by reading it, not assumed — so every one of those had to accept `any`, or an ORB trade could fire that Breakout had refused and the two would diverge **silently**. What stays binding is the window, the 1m acceptance, and whether the trade can physically be placed: the ORB, minus the retest. `check_breakout_research` R6 tests exactly that through the real trigger machinery.
🔴 **orb_range WAS MEASURED ANTI-SELECTIVE, n=747 — THE DOMINANT FILTER AND THE ONLY CANDIDATE BAND WORSE THAN NO GATE AT ALL.** The old band (0.05%–1.25%) refused **38%** of breaks and **kept the worse half**: 47.3% (0.94x) inside against 55.7% (1.10x) outside. ⚠️ **AND NO BAND IS AN EDGE** — best of seven candidates 1.08x on n=302 (~1.4 SE), the volume story again. The fitted band becomes feasibility-only (0.23%–3.5%, measured **1.01x**, neutral, which is what a feasibility gate should score). **A funnel replay first asked the question that mattered — does a setup ever appear at all** — because a plan that never forms is indistinguishable from one correctly declining, and this repo has been bitten by that twice (the dead FOMC detector, `_flow_conn`). **43.6% of 351 sessions survived the replayable bars**, so Breakout was alive; `orb_range` rejected 137 of them and was the binding constraint.
🔴 **AND "SIZED THE SAME AS THE ORB" WAS NOT TRUE — GEOMETRY SIZING WAS GATED BY A STRATEGY-NAME CHECK.** `main.py` read `if signal.strategy_name == "ORBStrategy"`, so Breakout fell through to `_size_budget`, **the restrictive rule**, and nothing said so because both paths return a `SizingResult`. ⚠️ **`RiskManager.size_for`'s OWN DOCSTRING FORBIDS THAT LIST:** *"Geometry is a sub-rule of long_debit, selected by the caller SUPPLYING orb_width / orb_stop_distance rather than by naming ORB. A second strategy that wants risk-normalised sizing supplies the geometry; it does not get added to a list somewhere that later rots."* The sizer refused a name list and **the caller kept one anyway**. Now keyed on an explicit `sizes_on_geometry` declaration — **NOT on `orb_range_high`, because the hunt and the runaway both carry that field** and neither asked to be re-sized (R8 pins it).
🔑 **AND THE DECLARED EXIT DESCRIBED AN EXIT THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** `EXIT_CONDITIONS["Breakout"]["structure_stop"]` read *"a 1m close back inside the opening range — the break failed"*, but both evaluation paths read `underlying_stop` — which for a Breakout IS the impulsive candle's extreme. Operator: *"If it comes back into the range but doesn't trigger a stop, make it HOLD."* **The code already did; the file did not say so** — and that string is what the board narrates and what the next editor implements against. Corrected to the ORB's wording verbatim. **The no-second-breakout half was already in place** (`max_open_of_type=1`, r50's carve-out for a strategy declining to stack on itself).
⚠️ **THE HONEST LIMIT, STATED BEFORE THE TWO WEEKS START:** one box, one symbol, ~87% of sessions producing a tradeable break = **about 9 trades**. The flow dial that landed at r53 used **n=255**; the pool finding at n=46 was refused a threshold at ~2 SE. **Nine observations cannot fit a dial.** Two weeks will prove the machinery runs and give real execution evidence — fills, slippage, whether the exhaustion exit behaves — and the FITTING still comes from the warehouse, except for `gamma_regime` and `depth_thin`, which have no history and can only ever be learned forward.
**MEASUREMENT: 122 of 132, zero regressions.** `check_breakout_research` BORN RED at base — and it CRASHED there first, the **FIFTH** time (r32, r37, r39, r41, r51), because the guard covered imports and not module attributes; a blanket net now converts any escape into a FAIL line, since a checker that cannot fail gracefully cannot prove it was born red. breakout v1.2, breakout_plan v1.1, management v2.6, main v4.58.

**Operator, 2026-09-18:** *"if something other than the window made it inactive it should declare that, but if it's the window, I just need to see that once not repeatedly."* WINDOW is announced **once per inactive episode** — an episode ending when the plan next goes WORKING — and every other gate (cap, type cap, tries, blocked_by) declares itself.

Menu item **PLAN STATUS NOW**, beside the existing PLAN BOARD, which is a chosen day's history. ⚠️ And the board immediately surfaced something per-tick logging buries: the `/manage` plans' last known status was **922 and 1,972 minutes old**.

**v0.39 — 2026-09-18 — OTV4TEST r36 — THE LEVEL BOARD IS BUILT FROM THE HOURLY TAPE AND REACHES TWELVE WEEKS; THE UNDERLYING'S OWN QUOTE IS FINALLY SUBSCRIBED.**
**LVL.12 ✅ · FEED.2 ✅ · LVL.13 still open (zones).** The operator read his own 1D chart against the live board and three of his five levels above spot **did not exist in the ledger at all**. Cause: `load_tape` read 1m, and retention keeps 1m for FIVE DAYS while keeping 1h for SIXTY — so the board reached nine days back while twelve weeks of hourly history sat unread in the same store. **Not missing data. UNREAD data** (§39.2). Reach now 2026-06-25 → today, **43→42 held levels of which 25 are Asia or London**, and all five of his levels resolve within 4–31 cents.
🔴 **DAILIES WERE TRIED, MEASURED AND RULED OUT.** They cannot see overnight: merged `1d` missed the true 24h low by **$2.33 on average and $9.13 at worst**, because DXFeed aggregates a daily candle over trading hours and the `_EXT` daily bar is a near-copy of the RTH one. Operator: *"the outside RTH levels are the ones I'm most expecting to get swept. It's imperative that we know where those levels sit."* `daily_levels`/`load_daily` were written, measured and **deleted in the same revision**. ⚠️ And the daily reader carried a defect worth remembering: it took the trading date from the stamp's **ET** date, but DXFeed stamps a daily candle at **00:00 UTC of its trading date**, so every level came out one session early — proven wrong 7 of 7. His rule is TIME FIRST, so that would not have been cosmetic.
🔑 **WHY THE HOUR LOSES NOTHING:** an hourly bar's high/low is an AGGREGATE, not a sample. Measured: merged 1h equals merged 1m on the 24h high AND low on every fully-covered day, and the mapper's sections are already hour-granular (`Asia 00-08`, `London 08-13`, `NY 13-20` UTC, membership on `idx.hour`). ⚠️ **WHAT IT COSTS, NAMED NOT DISCOVERED:** the reconcile runs once per new tape bar, so a level accepted through at 10:05 now retires when the 10:00 bar closes. The rejection FACT is untouched — `_derive_events` reads `ctx["df_1m"]`, the closed MINUTE — and the opening-range TRAVERSED rule still runs at TICK rate, so "removed immediately" holds literally.
🔴 **AND I CORRUPTED THE LIVE STORE ON THE WAY, FOUND IT WITH THE CHECKER, AND REPAIRED IT.** The first seed wrote 1,265 S3 hourly bars over locally-complete ones with `INSERT OR REPLACE` **without checking whether S3's version described a FINISHED hour. 369 of them — 29% — were PARTIAL**, pushed mid-hour: `09-16 19:00 UTC` read `705.46/704.08` when the true hour was `706.02/700.00`, so **a level at 700.00 that price turned at was invisible**. It was not a CDC dedupe failure (every S3 bar has exactly one version) — it was a blind overwrite of local data by a remote copy never compared to it. §38.1 names one path for warehouse reads so this class cannot be improvised, and I improvised. **REPAIR:** the series deleted and rebuilt from complete objects only (`pushed_at_utc >= bar_end`), then widened where local 1m saw more — 1,017 bars, and **217 of 218 hourly bars now match their own hour of 1m exactly** (the one exception is wider, from an hour our 1m only partly covers). No trade affected: market closed, nothing read the hourly tape in a decision, `trades.db` untouched.
**FEED.2 CLOSED:** r64's manifold took four of five streams for the ticker and left Quote, because Quote *was* subscribed — per CONTRACT. Measured: **0 of 22,846,560 `quote_series` rows were a non-option symbol.** It is the only source of RESTING DEPTH, and the operator's thesis — that size parks at coarse levels and is not worried about minute granularity — cannot be tested without it. ⚠️ **AND THE FIRST TEST OF THAT THESIS DID NOT CONFIRM:** volume within ±$0.25 of a session extreme carries a lift of **1.02x** against a random price in the traded band, i.e. none — but volume is the wrong instrument, because a level that HELD is one price turned AWAY from, so volume AT it conflates absorption with traversal. Collection only (§31); nothing reads it.
**GATE:** `tests/check_level_tape.py` T1–T7, born red at r35 on T1/T1b. **T2 is the one that caught the corruption** — every hourly bar against its OWN hour of 1m, 189 judged, 0 narrower. ⚠️ Two faults in my own checker, recorded: T6 matched the DOCSTRING instead of the code (§21, in a checker), and T2's first cut bucketed by UTC DAY and could not say which bar was wrong.
